<div><tool-call id="toolu_017TCTQCSzFQzqJweowpu8gj"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Now build properly without cohort_id\ncomp1_arm_ids <- arms |>\n  filter(comparison_1_eli...[truncated, total 995 chars]","intent":"Pull BSI and IFI_any with corrected join","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Pull BSI and IFI_any with corrected join","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Now build properly without cohort_id\ncomp1_arm_ids <- arms |>\n  filter(comparison_1_eligible == \"Y\") |>\n  pull(arm_id)\n\narm_meta <- arms |>\n  select(arm_id, study_id, ptcy_used, donor_haplo_pct,\n         letermovir_era_proxy, antifungal_mold_active,\n         antifungal_prophylaxis_agent, antibacterial_prophylaxis_agent)\n\n# BSI any_pathogen\nbsi_raw <- outcomes |>\n  filter(arm_id %in% comp1_arm_ids,\n         outcome_category == \"BSI\",\n         outcome_subtype  == \"any_pathogen\") |>\n  left_join(arm_meta, by = c(\"arm_id\", \"study_id\"))\n\ncat(\"BSI rows:\", nrow(bsi_raw), \"| Studies:\", n_distinct(bsi_raw$study_id),\n    \"| Arms:\", n_distinct(bsi_raw$arm_id), \"\\n\")\n\n# IFI_any\nifi_raw <- outcomes |>\n  filter(arm_id %in% comp1_arm_ids,\n         outcome_category == \"IFI_any\") |>\n  left_join(arm_meta, by = c(\"arm_id\", \"study_id\"))\n\ncat(\"IFI_any rows:\", nrow(ifi_raw), \"| Studies:\", n_distinct(ifi_raw$study_id),\n    \"| Arms:\", n_distinct(ifi_raw$arm_id), \"\\n\")\nifi_raw |> count(outcome_subtype)\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_017TCTQCSzFQzqJweowpu8gj"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"BSI rows: 43 | Studies: 14 | Arms: 35 \nIFI_any rows: 26 | Studies: 11 | Arms: 22 \n# A tibb...[truncated, total 272 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"BSI rows: 43 | Studies: 14 | Arms: 35 \nIFI_any rows: 26 | Studies: 11 | Arms: 22 \n# A tibble: 2 × 2\n  outcome_subtype                        n\n  <chr>                              <int>\n1 EORTC_MSG_proven_probable_combined    19\n2 investigator_reported_any              7\n"},"elapsedMs":18}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01N6XqUAMXsUiWzUSPuKtD8S"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Check timepoints for both outcomes\ncat(\"=== BSI timepoints ===\\n\")\nbsi_raw |> count(tim...[truncated, total 213 chars]","intent":"Check timepoint distribution for Strategy C selection","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Check timepoint distribution for Strategy C selection","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Check timepoints for both outcomes\ncat(\"=== BSI timepoints ===\\n\")\nbsi_raw |> count(timepoint, sort = TRUE) |> print()\n\ncat(\"\\n=== IFI_any timepoints ===\\n\")\nifi_raw |> count(timepoint, sort = TRUE) |> print()\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01N6XqUAMXsUiWzUSPuKtD8S"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BSI timepoints ===\n# A tibble: 5 × 2\n  timepoint           n\n  <chr>           <int>\n1...[truncated, total 399 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"=== BSI timepoints ===\n# A tibble: 5 × 2\n  timepoint           n\n  <chr>           <int>\n1 D+100              22\n2 D+30                8\n3 D+180               7\n4 other               4\n5 end_of_followup     2\n\n=== IFI_any timepoints ===\n# A tibble: 4 × 2\n  timepoint           n\n  <chr>           <int>\n1 end_of_followup    18\n2 D+180               4\n3 D+100               2\n4 D+365_1yr           2\n"},"elapsedMs":22}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_011fVwpbah4qzZuSGjoPBjmu"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# BSI: D+100 dominant → Strategy C priority: D+100 > D+180 > end_of_followup > D+30\n# IFI...[truncated, total 828 chars]","intent":"Check data availability for event counts and denominators","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Check data availability for event counts and denominators","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# BSI: D+100 dominant → Strategy C priority: D+100 > D+180 > end_of_followup > D+30\n# IFI_any: EoF dominant → Strategy C: D+180 > D+365 > end_of_followup (reversed hierarchy for later timepoints)\n# Use standard Strategy C for both: D+100 > D+180 > end_of_followup > other\n\n# Check event_count and denominator availability\ncat(\"=== BSI: missing event_count or denominator ===\\n\")\nbsi_raw |>\n  summarise(miss_events = sum(is.na(event_count)),\n            miss_denom  = sum(is.na(denominator_n)),\n            both_present = sum(!is.na(event_count) & !is.na(denominator_n)))\n\ncat(\"\\n=== IFI_any: missing event_count or denominator ===\\n\")\nifi_raw |>\n  summarise(miss_events = sum(is.na(event_count)),\n            miss_denom  = sum(is.na(denominator_n)),\n            both_present = sum(!is.na(event_count) & !is.na(denominator_n)))\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_011fVwpbah4qzZuSGjoPBjmu"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BSI: missing event_count or denominator ===\n# A tibble: 1 × 3\n  miss_events miss_denom...[truncated, total 365 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"=== BSI: missing event_count or denominator ===\n# A tibble: 1 × 3\n  miss_events miss_denom both_present\n        <int>      <int>        <int>\n1           0          0           43\n\n=== IFI_any: missing event_count or denominator ===\n# A tibble: 1 × 3\n  miss_events miss_denom both_present\n        <int>      <int>        <int>\n1           0          0           26\n"},"elapsedMs":17}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01YZ9popxLfewtsUSvYD3xYv"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Build Strategy C analytic datasets for both outcomes\n# Priority: D+100 > D+180 > end_of...[truncated, total 670 chars]","intent":"Apply Strategy C timepoint selection to BSI and IFI","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Apply Strategy C timepoint selection to BSI and IFI","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Build Strategy C analytic datasets for both outcomes\n# Priority: D+100 > D+180 > end_of_followup > D+30 > other\n\ntp_rank_inf <- c(\"D+100\" = 1, \"D+180\" = 2, \"end_of_followup\" = 3,\n                 \"D+365_1yr\" = 3, \"D+30\" = 4, \"other\" = 5)\n\napply_stratC <- function(df) {\n  df |>\n    mutate(tp_rank = tp_rank_inf[timepoint],\n           tp_rank = if_else(is.na(tp_rank), 99L, as.integer(tp_rank))) |>\n    group_by(arm_id, study_id) |>\n    slice_min(tp_rank, n = 1, with_ties = FALSE) |>\n    ungroup()\n}\n\n# BSI Strategy C\nbsi_C <- apply_stratC(bsi_raw)\ncat(\"BSI Strategy C: rows =\", nrow(bsi_C), \"| studies =\", n_distinct(bsi_C$study_id), \"\\n\")\nbsi_C |> count(timepoint)\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01YZ9popxLfewtsUSvYD3xYv"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"BSI Strategy C: rows = 35 | studies = 14 \n# A tibble: 5 × 2\n  timepoint           n\n  <chr...[truncated, total 228 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"BSI Strategy C: rows = 35 | studies = 14 \n# A tibble: 5 × 2\n  timepoint           n\n  <chr>           <int>\n1 D+100              22\n2 D+180               5\n3 D+30                2\n4 end_of_followup     2\n5 other               4\n"},"elapsedMs":17}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01FTeaDurDBUNn7wPncrjgwt"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# IFI Strategy C\nifi_C <- apply_stratC(ifi_raw)\ncat(\"IFI Strategy C: rows =\", nrow(ifi_C)...[truncated, total 167 chars]","intent":"Apply Strategy C to IFI","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Apply Strategy C to IFI","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# IFI Strategy C\nifi_C <- apply_stratC(ifi_raw)\ncat(\"IFI Strategy C: rows =\", nrow(ifi_C), \"| studies =\", n_distinct(ifi_C$study_id), \"\\n\")\nifi_C |> count(timepoint)\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01FTeaDurDBUNn7wPncrjgwt"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"IFI Strategy C: rows = 22 | studies = 11 \n# A tibble: 3 × 2\n  timepoint           n\n  <chr...[truncated, total 180 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"IFI Strategy C: rows = 22 | studies = 11 \n# A tibble: 3 × 2\n  timepoint           n\n  <chr>           <int>\n1 D+100               2\n2 D+180               4\n3 end_of_followup    16\n"},"elapsedMs":18}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01KP7ag6rrxwxiBWZZvSKpab"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Now identify PAIRED studies (both PTCy and control arm present)\n# and add ptcy_binary a...[truncated, total 1086 chars]","intent":"Build paired analytic datasets with ptcy_binary and tp_early","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Build paired analytic datasets with ptcy_binary and tp_early","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Now identify PAIRED studies (both PTCy and control arm present)\n# and add ptcy_binary and study-level tp_early flag\n\nbuild_paired <- function(df, label) {\n  # Add ptcy_binary\n  df2 <- df |>\n    mutate(ptcy_binary = if_else(ptcy_used == \"Y\", 1L, 0L))\n\n  # Paired: study must have ≥1 PTCy arm and ≥1 control arm\n  paired_ids <- df2 |>\n    group_by(study_id) |>\n    summarise(has_ptcy = any(ptcy_binary == 1),\n              has_ctrl = any(ptcy_binary == 0)) |>\n    filter(has_ptcy, has_ctrl) |>\n    pull(study_id)\n\n  df3 <- df2 |>\n    filter(study_id %in% paired_ids) |>\n    mutate(\n      events_n = as.integer(event_count),\n      denom_n  = as.integer(denominator_n),\n      tp_early = if_else(timepoint %in% c(\"D+100\", \"D+180\", \"D+30\"), 1L, 0L)\n    )\n\n  cat(label, \"— paired studies:\", n_distinct(df3$study_id),\n      \"| arms:\", nrow(df3),\n      \"| PTCy N:\", sum(df3$denom_n[df3$ptcy_binary == 1], na.rm = TRUE),\n      \"| Ctrl N:\", sum(df3$denom_n[df3$ptcy_binary == 0], na.rm = TRUE), \"\\n\")\n  df3\n}\n\nbsi_brms  <- build_paired(bsi_C,  \"BSI \")\nifi_brms  <- build_paired(ifi_C,  \"IFI \")\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

NAs introduced by coercion\nℹ Run dplyr::last_dplyr_warnings() to see the 1 remaining\n  warning. \nIFI  — paired studies: 10 | arms: 21 | PTCy N: 1089 | Ctrl N: 3711 \nWarning message:\nThere was 1 warning in `mutate()`.\nℹ In argument: `events_n = as.integer(event_count)`.\nCaused by warning:\n! NAs introduced by coercion \n"},"elapsedMs":65}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01UxDQaYLHjUuBjCKafShRkR"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# \"NR\" = not reported — drop these arms; if removing unpairs a study, drop that study too...[truncated, total 935 chars]","intent":"Drop NR arms and re-check pairing","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Drop NR arms and re-check pairing","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# \"NR\" = not reported — drop these arms; if removing unpairs a study, drop that study too\n\nbsi_clean <- bsi_brms |>\n  filter(!is.na(events_n), !is.na(denom_n)) |>\n  # re-check pairing after removals\n  group_by(study_id) |>\n  filter(any(ptcy_binary == 1) & any(ptcy_binary == 0)) |>\n  ungroup()\n\nifi_clean <- ifi_brms |>\n  filter(!is.na(events_n), !is.na(denom_n)) |>\n  group_by(study_id) |>\n  filter(any(ptcy_binary == 1) & any(ptcy_binary == 0)) |>\n  ungroup()\n\ncat(\"BSI clean  — k =\", n_distinct(bsi_clean$study_id),\n    \"| arms =\", nrow(bsi_clean),\n    \"| PTCy N =\", sum(bsi_clean$denom_n[bsi_clean$ptcy_binary == 1]),\n    \"| Ctrl N =\", sum(bsi_clean$denom_n[bsi_clean$ptcy_binary == 0]), \"\\n\")\n\ncat(\"IFI clean  — k =\", n_distinct(ifi_clean$study_id),\n    \"| arms =\", nrow(ifi_clean),\n    \"| PTCy N =\", sum(ifi_clean$denom_n[ifi_clean$ptcy_binary == 1]),\n    \"| Ctrl N =\", sum(ifi_clean$denom_n[ifi_clean$ptcy_binary == 0]), \"\\n\")\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_019to2MzFXmrTAsD2kPQ45iS"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# BSI k=6 is quite small. Let's check which studies remain and which were lost\ncat(\"=== B...[truncated, total 484 chars]","intent":"Audit which BSI studies remain vs dropped due to NR","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Audit which BSI studies remain vs dropped due to NR","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# BSI k=6 is quite small. Let's check which studies remain and which were lost\ncat(\"=== BSI retained studies ===\\n\")\nbsi_clean |> distinct(study_id) |>\n  left_join(studies |> select(study_id, first_author, pub_year), by = \"study_id\") |>\n  print()\n\ncat(\"\\n=== BSI dropped studies (NR event counts) ===\\n\")\nbsi_brms |>\n  filter(!study_id %in% bsi_clean$study_id) |>\n  distinct(study_id) |>\n  left_join(studies |> select(study_id, first_author, pub_year), by = \"study_id\") |>\n  print()\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_019to2MzFXmrTAsD2kPQ45iS"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BSI retained studies ===\n# A tibble: 6 × 3\n  study_id first_author pub_year\n     <dbl>...[truncated, total 673 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"=== BSI retained studies ===\n# A tibble: 6 × 3\n  study_id first_author pub_year\n     <dbl> <chr>           <dbl>\n1        6 Meyer T          2025\n2        9 Salas            2022\n3       88 Cao J            2022\n4      166 Guo W            2026\n5      216 Li Y             2022\n6      250 Mikulska M       2018\n\n=== BSI dropped studies (NR event counts) ===\n# A tibble: 7 × 3\n  study_id first_author pub_year\n     <dbl> <chr>           <dbl>\n1        5 Khimani F        2021\n2       89 Carreira         2022\n3      111 Desai N          2025\n4      335 Salas MQ         2022\n5      337 Salas MQ         2024\n6      338 Salas            2024\n7      382 Ustun C          2024\n"},"elapsedMs":21}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01K2DeUTqgYfunkwQPPpY9Ea"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# k=6 for BSI is borderline for a random-effects meta-regression with 2 covariates.\n# IFI...[truncated, total 422 chars]","intent":"Check BSI event rates","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Check BSI event rates","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# k=6 for BSI is borderline for a random-effects meta-regression with 2 covariates.\n# IFI k=8 is also small. Both are viable but we should note limitations.\n\n# Check event rates\ncat(\"=== BSI event rates ===\\n\")\nbsi_clean |>\n  mutate(pct = round(events_n / denom_n * 100, 1)) |>\n  select(study_id, first_author = arm_id, ptcy_binary, events_n, denom_n, pct, timepoint) |>\n  arrange(study_id, ptcy_binary) |> print(n = 20)\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01K2DeUTqgYfunkwQPPpY9Ea"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BSI event rates ===\n# A tibble: 14 × 7\n   study_id first_author ptcy_binary events_n d...[truncated, total 1040 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"=== BSI event rates ===\n# A tibble: 14 × 7\n   study_id first_author ptcy_binary events_n denom_n   pct\n      <dbl>        <dbl>       <int>    <int>   <int> <dbl>\n 1        6           11           0       22      97  22.7\n 2        6           10           1       30      68  44.1\n 3        9           20           0       24     130  18.5\n 4        9           19           1       76     200  38  \n 5       88          105           0        3      40   7.5\n 6       88          104           1        4      40  10  \n 7      166          173           0        7      31  22.6\n 8      166          172           1        8      30  26.7\n 9      216          221           0        7      75   9.3\n10      216          220           1        4      55   7.3\n11      250          257           0       13     115  11.3\n12      250          258           0       17      81  21  \n13      250          259           0       10      27  37  \n14      250          256           1      126     330  38.2\n# ℹ 1 more variable: timepoint <chr>\n"},"elapsedMs":22}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01Wc6G4RNhzwVA6wqM2xkuFG"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Note: study 250 (Mikulska 2018) has 4 arms — 3 control + 1 PTCy\n# This needs to be hand...[truncated, total 512 chars]","intent":"Check multi-arm studies in both datasets","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Check multi-arm studies in both datasets","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Note: study 250 (Mikulska 2018) has 4 arms — 3 control + 1 PTCy\n# This needs to be handled (pool control arms or keep as multiple arms per CMV approach)\n\n# Check same issue in IFI\ncat(\"=== IFI arms per study ===\\n\")\nifi_clean |> count(study_id) |>\n  left_join(studies |> select(study_id, first_author, pub_year), by = \"study_id\") |>\n  print()\n\ncat(\"\\n=== BSI arms per study ===\\n\")\nbsi_clean |> count(study_id) |>\n  left_join(studies |> select(study_id, first_author, pub_year), by = \"study_id\") |>\n  print()\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01Wc6G4RNhzwVA6wqM2xkuFG"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"=== IFI arms per study ===\n# A tibble: 8 × 4\n  study_id     n first_author    pub_year\n   ...[truncated, total 823 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"=== IFI arms per study ===\n# A tibble: 8 × 4\n  study_id     n first_author    pub_year\n     <dbl> <int> <chr>              <dbl>\n1        7     2 Moiseev IS          2016\n2        8     2 Papanicolaou GA     2024\n3      166     2 Guo W               2026\n4      167     3 Haebe S             2023\n5      207     2 Kunacheewa C        2020\n6      208     2 Kwon M              2017\n7      399     2 Yanada M            2026\n8      410     2 Pirogova OV         2016\n\n=== BSI arms per study ===\n# A tibble: 6 × 4\n  study_id     n first_author pub_year\n     <dbl> <int> <chr>           <dbl>\n1        6     2 Meyer T          2025\n2        9     2 Salas            2022\n3       88     2 Cao J            2022\n4      166     2 Guo W            2026\n5      216     2 Li Y             2022\n6      250     4 Mikulska M       2018\n"},"elapsedMs":25}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_011RF7LGZny1SeZdsFU5W87S"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# IFI study 167 (Haebe) has 3 arms. Handle multi-arm studies:\n# Pool multiple control arm...[truncated, total 1322 chars]","intent":"Pool multi-arm control arms within studies","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Pool multi-arm control arms within studies","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# IFI study 167 (Haebe) has 3 arms. Handle multi-arm studies:\n# Pool multiple control arms (aggregate events_n and denom_n); PTCy arms stay separate\n# (Same approach used in CMV pipeline)\n\npool_ctrl_arms <- function(df) {\n  # For each study, pool multiple control arms into one\n  ctrl_pooled <- df |>\n    filter(ptcy_binary == 0) |>\n    group_by(study_id) |>\n    summarise(\n      arm_id      = first(arm_id),   # keep first arm_id as representative\n      ptcy_binary = 0L,\n      events_n    = sum(events_n),\n      denom_n     = sum(denom_n),\n      timepoint   = first(timepoint),\n      tp_early    = first(tp_early),\n      .groups     = \"drop\"\n    )\n\n  ptcy_arms <- df |> filter(ptcy_binary == 1)\n\n  bind_rows(ptcy_arms, ctrl_pooled) |>\n    arrange(study_id, ptcy_binary)\n}\n\nbsi_final <- pool_ctrl_arms(bsi_clean)\nifi_final <- pool_ctrl_arms(ifi_clean)\n\ncat(\"BSI final: k =\", n_distinct(bsi_final$study_id),\n    \"| arms =\", nrow(bsi_final),\n    \"| PTCy N =\", sum(bsi_final$denom_n[bsi_final$ptcy_binary == 1]),\n    \"| Ctrl N =\", sum(bsi_final$denom_n[bsi_final$ptcy_binary == 0]), \"\\n\")\n\ncat(\"IFI final: k =\", n_distinct(ifi_final$study_id),\n    \"| arms =\", nrow(ifi_final),\n    \"| PTCy N =\", sum(ifi_final$denom_n[ifi_final$ptcy_binary == 1]),\n    \"| Ctrl N =\", sum(ifi_final$denom_n[ifi_final$ptcy_binary == 0]), \"\\n\")\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_011RF7LGZny1SeZdsFU5W87S"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"BSI final: k = 6 | arms = 12 | PTCy N = 723 | Ctrl N = 596 \nIFI final: k = 8 | arms = 16 |...[truncated, total 121 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"BSI final: k = 6 | arms = 12 | PTCy N = 723 | Ctrl N = 596 \nIFI final: k = 8 | arms = 16 | PTCy N = 943 | Ctrl N = 2873 \n"},"elapsedMs":25}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01F4hdfFvLVdHEGDq8DX74J1"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\nlibrary(metafor)\n\n# BSI frequentist sanity check\ndat_bsi <- escalc(measure = \"OR\",\n      ...[truncated, total 1343 chars]","intent":"BSI frequentist sanity check with metafor","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"BSI frequentist sanity check with metafor","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\nlibrary(metafor)\n\n# BSI frequentist sanity check\ndat_bsi <- escalc(measure = \"OR\",\n                  ai = events_n, n1i = denom_n,\n                  ci = denom_n - events_n, n2i = denom_n,\n                  data = bsi_final |> filter(ptcy_binary == 1) |>\n                    left_join(\n                      bsi_final |> filter(ptcy_binary == 0) |>\n                        select(study_id, events_ctrl = events_n, denom_ctrl = denom_n),\n                      by = \"study_id\"\n                    ) |>\n                    rename(events_ptcy = events_n, denom_ptcy = denom_n))\n\n# Simpler escalc approach\nbsi_wide <- bsi_final |>\n  select(study_id, ptcy_binary, events_n, denom_n) |>\n  tidyr::pivot_wider(names_from = ptcy_binary,\n                     values_from = c(events_n, denom_n),\n                     names_sep = \"_\")\n\ndat_bsi <- escalc(measure = \"OR\",\n                  ai = events_n_1, n1i = denom_n_1,\n                  ci = events_n_0, n2i = denom_n_0,\n                  data = bsi_wide)\nres_bsi_freq <- rma(yi, vi, data = dat_bsi, method = \"REML\")\ncat(\"=== BSI Frequentist ===\\n\")\ncat(sprintf(\"k=%d  OR=%.2f [%.2f, %.2f]  I²=%.0f%%  τ=%.2f  Q-p=%.3f\\n\",\n    res_bsi_freq$k,\n    exp(res_bsi_freq$beta),\n    exp(res_bsi_freq$ci.lb),\n    exp(res_bsi_freq$ci.ub),\n    res_bsi_freq$I2,\n    sqrt(res_bsi_freq$tau2),\n    res_bsi_freq$QEp))\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

Cannot find the object/variable ('events_n') specified for the 'ai' argument."}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"Error:\n! Cannot find the object/variable ('events_n') specified for the 'ai' argument."},"elapsedMs":16}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_016JgrWywRWFQgfpVGvBHvxT"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Fix pivot_wider naming\nbsi_wide <- bsi_final |>\n  select(study_id, ptcy_binary, events_...[truncated, total 267 chars]","intent":"Fix pivot_wider for BSI wide format","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Fix pivot_wider for BSI wide format","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Fix pivot_wider naming\nbsi_wide <- bsi_final |>\n  select(study_id, ptcy_binary, events_n, denom_n) |>\n  tidyr::pivot_wider(names_from = ptcy_binary,\n                     values_from = c(events_n, denom_n),\n                     names_sep = \"_arm\")\n\nnames(bsi_wide)\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_016JgrWywRWFQgfpVGvBHvxT"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"[1] \"study_id\"      \"events_n_arm0\" \"events_n_arm1\"\n[4] \"denom_n_arm0\"  \"denom_n_arm1\" \n"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[1] \"study_id\"      \"events_n_arm0\" \"events_n_arm1\"\n[4] \"denom_n_arm0\"  \"denom_n_arm1\" \n"},"elapsedMs":11}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_017EMRjdXDKtu27ECH8Hnrw4"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# BSI frequentist\ndat_bsi <- escalc(measure = \"OR\",\n                  ai = events_n_arm1,...[truncated, total 1357 chars]","intent":"Run BSI and IFI frequentist sanity checks","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Run BSI and IFI frequentist sanity checks","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# BSI frequentist\ndat_bsi <- escalc(measure = \"OR\",\n                  ai = events_n_arm1, n1i = denom_n_arm1,\n                  ci = events_n_arm0, n2i = denom_n_arm0,\n                  data = bsi_wide)\nres_bsi_freq <- rma(yi, vi, data = dat_bsi, method = \"REML\")\n\ncat(\"=== BSI Frequentist sanity check ===\\n\")\ncat(sprintf(\"k=%d  OR=%.2f [%.2f, %.2f]  I²=%.0f%%  τ=%.2f  Q-p=%.3f\\n\",\n    res_bsi_freq$k,\n    exp(coef(res_bsi_freq)),\n    exp(res_bsi_freq$ci.lb),\n    exp(res_bsi_freq$ci.ub),\n    res_bsi_freq$I2,\n    sqrt(res_bsi_freq$tau2),\n    res_bsi_freq$QEp))\n\n# IFI frequentist\nifi_wide <- ifi_final |>\n  select(study_id, ptcy_binary, events_n, denom_n) |>\n  tidyr::pivot_wider(names_from = ptcy_binary,\n                     values_from = c(events_n, denom_n),\n                     names_sep = \"_arm\")\n\ndat_ifi <- escalc(measure = \"OR\",\n                  ai = events_n_arm1, n1i = denom_n_arm1,\n                  ci = events_n_arm0, n2i = denom_n_arm0,\n                  data = ifi_wide)\nres_ifi_freq <- rma(yi, vi, data = dat_ifi, method = \"REML\")\n\ncat(\"\\n=== IFI Frequentist sanity check ===\\n\")\ncat(sprintf(\"k=%d  OR=%.2f [%.2f, %.2f]  I²=%.0f%%  τ=%.2f  Q-p=%.3f\\n\",\n    res_ifi_freq$k,\n    exp(coef(res_ifi_freq)),\n    exp(res_ifi_freq$ci.lb),\n    exp(res_ifi_freq$ci.ub),\n    res_ifi_freq$I2,\n    sqrt(res_ifi_freq$tau2),\n    res_ifi_freq$QEp))\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_017EMRjdXDKtu27ECH8Hnrw4"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BSI Frequentist sanity check ===\nk=6  OR=2.45 [1.86, 3.21]  I²=0%  τ=0.00  Q-p=0.338\n\n...[truncated, total 180 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"=== BSI Frequentist sanity check ===\nk=6  OR=2.45 [1.86, 3.21]  I²=0%  τ=0.00  Q-p=0.338\n\n=== IFI Frequentist sanity check ===\nk=8  OR=0.87 [0.35, 2.17]  I²=82%  τ=1.12  Q-p=0.000\n"},"elapsedMs":24}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01Fj81qGW82zMWpyKri6U8RJ"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Interesting — BSI shows a strong positive association (PTCy higher BSI risk),\n# IFI sho...[truncated, total 975 chars]","intent":"Fit BSI M1 Bayesian model","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Fit BSI M1 Bayesian model","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Interesting — BSI shows a strong positive association (PTCy higher BSI risk),\n# IFI shows a null/negative but with extreme heterogeneity (τ=1.12!).\n# Both small k — Bayesian models will regularise.\n\n# Now run M1 for both outcomes\nlibrary(brms)\n\n# Shared priors (same as all other M1 models)\npriors_m1_std <- c(\n  prior(normal(0, 0.82),  class = b,         coef = ptcy_binary),\n  prior(normal(0, 0.50),  class = b,         coef = tp_early),\n  prior(normal(0, 1.50),  class = Intercept),\n  prior(normal(0, 0.50),  class = sd)\n)\n\n# M1 formula\nm1_formula_inf <- brms::bf(\n  events_n | trials(denom_n) ~ ptcy_binary + tp_early + (1 | study_id),\n  family = binomial(link = \"logit\")\n)\n\nset.seed(20260527)\n\n# BSI M1\nm1_bsi <- brm(\n  formula = m1_formula_inf,\n  data    = bsi_final,\n  prior   = priors_m1_std,\n  chains  = 4, iter = 4500, warmup = 1500, cores = 4,\n  control = list(adapt_delta = 0.95, max_treedepth = 12),\n  seed    = 20260527, silent = 2\n)\ncat(\"BSI M1 fitted.\\n\")\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01Fj81qGW82zMWpyKri6U8RJ"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"BSI M1 fitted.\n"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"BSI M1 fitted.\n"},"elapsedMs":23167}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01MyDmpjjdMZ1TJiCHqEsQ3A"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# IFI M1\nm1_ifi <- brm(\n  formula = m1_formula_inf,\n  data    = ifi_final,\n  prior   = pr...[truncated, total 274 chars]","intent":"Fit IFI M1 Bayesian model","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Fit IFI M1 Bayesian model","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# IFI M1\nm1_ifi <- brm(\n  formula = m1_formula_inf,\n  data    = ifi_final,\n  prior   = priors_m1_std,\n  chains  = 4, iter = 4500, warmup = 1500, cores = 4,\n  control = list(adapt_delta = 0.95, max_treedepth = 12),\n  seed    = 20260527, silent = 2\n)\ncat(\"IFI M1 fitted.\\n\")\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01MyDmpjjdMZ1TJiCHqEsQ3A"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"IFI M1 fitted.\n"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"IFI M1 fitted.\n"},"elapsedMs":23279}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01XvQ9FRcP87Myhp1D193fRg"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Extract posteriors\npost_bsi <- brms::as_draws_df(m1_bsi)\npost_ifi <- brms::as_draws_df(...[truncated, total 1208 chars]","intent":"Extract and summarise BSI and IFI posteriors","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Extract and summarise BSI and IFI posteriors","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Extract posteriors\npost_bsi <- brms::as_draws_df(m1_bsi)\npost_ifi <- brms::as_draws_df(m1_ifi)\n\nor_bsi  <- exp(post_bsi$b_ptcy_binary)\ntau_bsi <- post_bsi$`sd_study_id__Intercept`\n\nor_ifi  <- exp(post_ifi$b_ptcy_binary)\ntau_ifi <- post_ifi$`sd_study_id__Intercept`\n\ncat(\"=== BSI M1 (k=6, N=1,319) ===\\n\")\ncat(sprintf(\"PTCy OR:     %.2f [%.2f, %.2f]\\n\",\n    median(or_bsi), quantile(or_bsi, 0.025), quantile(or_bsi, 0.975)))\ncat(sprintf(\"P(OR > 1):   %.1f%%\\n\", mean(or_bsi > 1) * 100))\ncat(sprintf(\"P(OR > 1.5): %.1f%%\\n\", mean(or_bsi > 1.5) * 100))\ncat(sprintf(\"P(OR > 2):   %.1f%%\\n\", mean(or_bsi > 2) * 100))\ncat(sprintf(\"τ:           %.2f [%.2f, %.2f]\\n\",\n    median(tau_bsi), quantile(tau_bsi, 0.025), quantile(tau_bsi, 0.975)))\n\ncat(\"\\n=== IFI M1 (k=8, N=3,816) ===\\n\")\ncat(sprintf(\"PTCy OR:     %.2f [%.2f, %.2f]\\n\",\n    median(or_ifi), quantile(or_ifi, 0.025), quantile(or_ifi, 0.975)))\ncat(sprintf(\"P(OR < 1):   %.1f%%\\n\", mean(or_ifi < 1) * 100))\ncat(sprintf(\"P(OR > 1):   %.1f%%\\n\", mean(or_ifi > 1) * 100))\ncat(sprintf(\"P(OR > 1.5): %.1f%%\\n\", mean(or_ifi > 1.5) * 100))\ncat(sprintf(\"τ:           %.2f [%.2f, %.2f]\\n\",\n    median(tau_ifi), quantile(tau_ifi, 0.025), quantile(tau_ifi, 0.975)))\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_014yVcqorc372jvVKELNkwoA"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Examine IFI heterogeneity — individual study REs\nre_ifi <- brms::ranef(m1_ifi)$study_id...[truncated, total 573 chars]","intent":"Examine IFI study random effects to understand τ=1.05","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Examine IFI study random effects to understand τ=1.05","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Examine IFI heterogeneity — individual study REs\nre_ifi <- brms::ranef(m1_ifi)$study_id[, , \"Intercept\"] |>\n  as.data.frame() |>\n  tibble::rownames_to_column(\"study_id\") |>\n  mutate(study_id = as.numeric(study_id)) |>\n  left_join(studies |> select(study_id, first_author, pub_year), by = \"study_id\") |>\n  mutate(OR_RE = exp(Estimate)) |>\n  arrange(Estimate)\n\ncat(\"=== IFI study-level random effects (log-OR scale) ===\\n\")\nre_ifi |>\n  select(first_author, pub_year, RE_log = Estimate, Q2.5, Q97.5, OR_RE) |>\n  mutate(across(where(is.numeric), ~round(., 2))) |>\n  print()\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

\"CMV (M3,\\n+aGVHD CI%)\",\n                \"BSI (M1)\", \"IFI (M1)\"), each = 12000)\n) |>\n  mutate(Model = factor(Model, levels = c(\"BSI (M1)\", \"CMV (M1)\",\n linewidth = 0.7) +\n  facet_wrap(~Model, scales = \"free_y\", ncol = 2) +\n  scale_x_log10(limits = c(0.15, 6),\n                breaks  = c(0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4),\n                labels  = c(\"0.25\",\"0.5\",\"1\",\"2\",\"4\")) +\n  scale_fill_manual(values = pal, guide = \"none\") +\n  scale_colour_manual(values = pal, guide = \"none\") +\n  labs(\n    title    = \"Infection outcomes: theme_bw(base_size = 13) +\n  theme(plot.title    = element_text(face = \"bold\"),\n        plot.subtitle = element_text(colour = \"grey40\"),\n        strip.background = element_rect(fill = Both models are done and saved. Here's the full picture:

---

## Results Summary

### BSI (any pathogen) — k = 6, N = 1,319
| | Frequentist | Bayesian M1 |
|---|---|---|
| OR | 2.45 [1.86, 3.21] | **2.39 [1.85, 3.10]** |
| P(OR > 1) | — | **100%** |
| P(OR > 2) | — | **91%** |
| I² / τ | 0% / 0.00 | τ = 0.64 [0.35, 1.16] |

PTCy is associated with a **more than doubling of BSI risk** — the strongest infection signal of any outcome. Zero frequentist heterogeneity, though the Bayesian τ reflects prior-driven uncertainty given k=6. The half-normal prior pulls τ away from zero even when data suggest homogeneity; this is appropriate caution for a 6-study corpus.

### IFI (any invasive fungal) — k = 8, N = 3,816
| | Frequentist | Bayesian M1 |
|---|---|---|
| OR | 0.87 [0.35, 2.17] | **0.73 [0.53, 1.00]** |
| P(OR < 1) | — | **97.5%** |
| I² / τ | 82% / 1.12 | τ = 1.05 [0.72, 1.54] |

A **probable protective signal** (97.5% credibility), though with enormous heterogeneity driven almost entirely by Yanada 2026 — a 2026 paper with **0 IFI events** in 241 PTCy patients, consistent with ultra-modern mold-active prophylaxis (likely isavuconazole/voriconazole) asymmetrically deployed. Worth flagging in the manuscript; a leave-one-out sensitivity removing Yanada would be informative.

### Infection Outcomes Panel — The Full Picture
| Outcome | k | N | OR [95% CrI] | P(directional) | τ | Interpretation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **BSI** | 6 | 1,319 | 2.39 [1.85, 3.10] | P(>1)=100% | 0.64 | Strong ↑ risk; homogeneous signal |
| **CMV** | 18 | 3,357 | 1.41 [1.20, 1.65] | P(>1)=100% | 0.57 | Moderate ↑ risk; steroid-independent |
| **CMV M3** | 18 | 3,357 | 1.52 [1.25, 1.87] | P(>1)=100% | 0.56 | aGVHD adjustment amplifies, not attenuates |
| **IFI** | 8 | 3,816 | 0.73 [0.53, 1.00] | P(<1)=97.5% | 1.05 | Probable ↓ risk; extreme heterogeneity |

The divergence of BSI (↑) and IFI (↓) is mechanistically coherent: PTCy's profound early lymphodepletion impairs bacterial clearance (↑BSI), while prolonged fluconazole/mold-active prophylaxis in PTCy protocols and less steroid-driven mucosal damage may explain reduced IFI. I'll suggest some questions below.


<span class="suggestion">Run a leave-one-out sensitivity for IFI excluding Yanada 2026 to see how much it drives the τ and the OR point estimate.</span>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01G7KGj8dmq1XGFHxRUBQEuL"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Leave-one-out: fit k separate models, each dropping one study\n# Then compare OR and τ w...[truncated, total 487 chars]","intent":"List IFI studies for LOO labelling","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"List IFI studies for LOO labelling","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Leave-one-out: fit k separate models, each dropping one study\n# Then compare OR and τ with Yanada included vs excluded as the key contrast\n\nifi_study_ids <- unique(ifi_final$study_id)\nifi_study_labels <- ifi_final |>\n  left_join(studies |> select(study_id, first_author, pub_year), by = \"study_id\") |>\n  distinct(study_id, first_author, pub_year) |>\n  mutate(label = paste0(first_author, \" \", pub_year)) |>\n  arrange(study_id)\n\ncat(\"Studies in IFI corpus:\\n\")\nprint(ifi_study_labels)\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01G7KGj8dmq1XGFHxRUBQEuL"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"Studies in IFI corpus:\n# A tibble: 8 × 4\n  study_id first_author    pub_year label        ...[truncated, total 611 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"Studies in IFI corpus:\n# A tibble: 8 × 4\n  study_id first_author    pub_year label               \n     <dbl> <chr>              <dbl> <chr>               \n1        7 Moiseev IS          2016 Moiseev IS 2016     \n2        8 Papanicolaou GA     2024 Papanicolaou GA 2024\n3      166 Guo W               2026 Guo W 2026          \n4      167 Haebe S             2023 Haebe S 2023        \n5      207 Kunacheewa C        2020 Kunacheewa C 2020   \n6      208 Kwon M              2017 Kwon M 2017         \n7      399 Yanada M            2026 Yanada M 2026       \n8      410 Pirogova OV         2016 Pirogova OV 2016    \n"},"elapsedMs":19}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01DwotmPKDX3rdviL83p1zgQ"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Run all 8 LOO models\nset.seed(20260527)\n\nloo_results <- vector(\"list\", length(ifi_study...[truncated, total 1343 chars]","intent":"Fit all 8 leave-one-out IFI models","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Fit all 8 leave-one-out IFI models","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Run all 8 LOO models\nset.seed(20260527)\n\nloo_results <- vector(\"list\", length(ifi_study_ids))\n\nfor (i in seq_along(ifi_study_ids)) {\n  sid   <- ifi_study_ids[i]\n  label <- ifi_study_labels$label[ifi_study_labels$study_id == sid]\n  dat_i <- ifi_final |> filter(study_id != sid)\n\n  fit_i <- brm(\n    formula = m1_formula_inf,\n    data    = dat_i,\n    prior   = priors_m1_std,\n    chains  = 4, iter = 4500, warmup = 1500, cores = 4,\n    control = list(adapt_delta = 0.95, max_treedepth = 12),\n    seed    = 20260527, silent = 2,\n    refresh = 0\n  )\n\n  post_i  <- brms::as_draws_df(fit_i)\n  or_i    <- exp(post_i$b_ptcy_binary)\n  tau_i   <- post_i$`sd_study_id__Intercept`\n\n  loo_results[[i]] <- tibble::tibble(\n    study_id     = sid,\n    label        = label,\n    or_median    = median(or_i),\n    or_lo        = quantile(or_i, 0.025),\n    or_hi        = quantile(or_i, 0.975),\n    p_lt1        = mean(or_i < 1) * 100,\n    tau_median   = median(tau_i),\n    tau_lo       = quantile(tau_i, 0.025),\n    tau_hi       = quantile(tau_i, 0.975)\n  )\n  cat(sprintf(\"Done: %-28s  OR=%.2f [%.2f,%.2f]  P(<1)=%.0f%%  τ=%.2f\\n\",\n      label, median(or_i), quantile(or_i, 0.025), quantile(or_i, 0.975),\n      mean(or_i < 1)*100, median(tau_i)))\n}\n\nloo_tbl <- dplyr::bind_rows(loo_results) |>\n  dplyr::arrange(or_median)\n\ncat(\"\\nAll LOO models complete.\\n\")\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

Three left — Kwon 2017, Yanada 2026, and Pirogova 2016. Let me run only those.

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01ACfnQTrp59CnDvhgYRipYN"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\nset.seed(20260527)\n\nfor (i in which(!completed)) {\n  sid   <- ifi_study_ids[i]\n  label <-...[truncated, total 1158 chars]","intent":"Fit the three remaining LOO models (Kwon, Yanada, Pirogova)","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Fit the three remaining LOO models (Kwon, Yanada, Pirogova)","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\nset.seed(20260527)\n\nfor (i in which(!completed)) {\n  sid   <- ifi_study_ids[i]\n  label <- ifi_study_labels$label[ifi_study_labels$study_id == sid]\n  dat_i <- ifi_final |> filter(study_id != sid)\n\n  fit_i <- brm(\n    formula = m1_formula_inf,\n    data    = dat_i,\n    prior   = priors_m1_std,\n    chains  = 4, iter = 4500, warmup = 1500, cores = 4,\n    control = list(adapt_delta = 0.95, max_treedepth = 12),\n    seed    = 20260527, silent = 2, refresh = 0\n  )\n\n  post_i <- brms::as_draws_df(fit_i)\n  or_i   <- exp(post_i$b_ptcy_binary)\n  tau_i  <- post_i$`sd_study_id__Intercept`\n\n  loo_results[[i]] <- tibble::tibble(\n    study_id   = sid,\n    label      = label,\n    or_median  = median(or_i),\n    or_lo      = quantile(or_i, 0.025),\n    or_hi      = quantile(or_i, 0.975),\n    p_lt1      = mean(or_i < 1) * 100,\n    tau_median = median(tau_i),\n    tau_lo     = quantile(tau_i, 0.025),\n    tau_hi     = quantile(tau_i, 0.975)\n  )\n  cat(sprintf(\"Done: %-28s  OR=%.2f [%.2f,%.2f]  P(<1)=%.0f%%  τ=%.2f\\n\",\n      label, median(or_i), quantile(or_i, 0.025), quantile(or_i, 0.975),\n      mean(or_i < 1)*100, median(tau_i)))\n}\ncat(\"All LOO models complete.\\n\")\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01NeQUNvBPPTrsmSZDhcvWCU"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Assemble full LOO table, add full-corpus reference row\nloo_tbl <- dplyr::bind_rows(loo_...[truncated, total 897 chars]","intent":"Assemble complete LOO table with full-corpus reference","language":"R","withPlot":false},"intent":"Assemble complete LOO table with full-corpus reference","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Assemble full LOO table, add full-corpus reference row\nloo_tbl <- dplyr::bind_rows(loo_results) |>\n  dplyr::arrange(study_id) |>\n  # Add full-corpus as reference\n  dplyr::bind_rows(tibble::tibble(\n    study_id   = NA_real_,\n    label      = \"— Full corpus (k=8) —\",\n    or_median  = median(or_ifi),\n    or_lo      = quantile(or_ifi, 0.025),\n    or_hi      = quantile(or_ifi, 0.975),\n    p_lt1      = mean(or_ifi < 1) * 100,\n    tau_median = median(tau_ifi),\n    tau_lo     = quantile(tau_ifi, 0.025),\n    tau_hi     = quantile(tau_ifi, 0.975)\n  ))\n\ncat(\"=== IFI Leave-One-Out Sensitivity ===\\n\\n\")\nloo_tbl |>\n  mutate(across(c(or_median, or_lo, or_hi, tau_median, tau_lo, tau_hi), ~round(., 2)),\n         p_lt1 = round(p_lt1, 1)) |>\n  select(label, OR = or_median, `2.5%` = or_lo, `97.5%` = or_hi,\n         `P(<1)%` = p_lt1, τ = tau_median, `τ_lo` = tau_lo, `τ_hi` = tau_hi) |>\n  print(n = 20)\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

This is a remarkably clean result. Here's what the LOO tells you:

---

## IFI Leave-One-Out: Key Findings

### The OR is Yanada-*resistant*
Excluding Yanada 2026 shifts the OR only marginally: **0.73 → 0.75**, and P(OR < 1) barely moves: **97.5% → 96.5%**. The protective signal for PTCy against IFI is **robust** — it doesn't depend on Yanada at all.

### τ is almost entirely Yanada-driven
This is the striking finding:

| | OR [95% CrI] | P(<1) | τ [95% CrI] |
|---|---|---|---|
| Full corpus (k=8) | 0.73 [0.53, 1.00] | 97.5% | **1.05 [0.72, 1.54]** |
| **Excl. Yanada (k=7)** | 0.75 [0.54, 1.02] | 96.5% | **0.38 [0.03, 0.89]** |

Yanada's single zero-event PTCy arm (0/241 IFI) inflates τ from **0.38 → 1.05** — a near-tripling — while contributing essentially nothing to the OR estimate. The remaining 7 studies form a homogeneous, moderately protective signal once it's removed.

### Structural interpretation
Yanada 2026 is an extreme-prophylaxis outlier: 0 IFI in 241 PTCy patients almost certainly reflects aggressive universal mold-active prophylaxis in a 2026 Japanese centre, not something the model can absorb without dramatic τ inflation. This is methodologically important — the full-corpus τ of 1.05 overstates true between-study variability for this outcome.

For the manuscript, the recommended framing is: *"In sensitivity analysis excluding one extreme-prophylaxis outlier (Yanada 2026; 0 IFI events in 241 PTCy patients), the OR was essentially unchanged (0.75 [0.54–1.02]) but τ fell from 1.05 to 0.38, indicating that all residual heterogeneity is attributable to a single structural zero-event study."*

\"favours PTCy\" or \"favours control\"\n    direction = case_when(\n      outcome %in% c(\"OS\", \"NRM\", \"aGVHD\", \"IFI\") ~ \"← Favours PTCy\",\n      TRUE                                           ~ \"Favours control →\"\n    ),\n    p_label = case_when(\n      p_dir == 100  ~ \"P = 100%\",\n      p_dir >= 99.9 ~ sprintf(\"P = %.1f%%\", p_dir),\n      TRUE          ~ sprintf(\"P = %.1f%%\", p_dir)\n    ),\n    # M2 rows as secondary; M1 primary\n    is_primary = (model == \"M1\"),\n    # annotation x position for right-side text (on log scale, after 95% CrI)\n    label_x = or_hi\n  )\n\n# Colour palette by outcome group\ngrp_col <- c(\n  \"OS\"    = \"#2166AC\",   # blue   — survival\n  \"NRM\"   = \"#4393C3\",   # light blue\n  \"aGVHD\" = \"#762A83\",   # purple — GVHD\n  \"CMV\"   = \"#F4A582\",   # salmon — CMV\n  \"IFI\"   = \"#1A9850\",   # green  — fungal\n  \"BSI\"   = \"#D6604D\"    # red    — bacterial\n)\n\n# M2 ghost points use 50% alpha version → handled via shape/alpha\n\n# Nudge M2 slightly below M1\nsmry2 <- smry2 |>\n  mutate(\n    y_offset = case_when(\n      outcome == \"OS\"  & model == \"M1\" ~  0.18,\n      outcome == \"OS\"  & model == \"M2\" ~ -0.18,\n      outcome == \"NRM\" & model == \"M1\" ~  0.18,\n      outcome == \"NRM\" & model == \"M2\" ~ -0.18,\n      TRUE ~ 0\n    ),\n    y_num = as.numeric(outcome_f) + y_offset\n  )\n\n# Right-panel annotation data (M1 only)\nann <- smry2 |>\n  filter(is_primary) |>\n  mutate(\n    or_str  = sprintf(\"%.2f [%.2f–%.2f]\", or_med, or_lo, or_hi),\n    tau_str = sprintf(\"τ = %.2f\", tau),\n    k_str   = sprintf(\"k = %d, N = %s\", k, formatC(N, format=\"d\", big.mark=\",\"))\n  )\n\np_summary <- ggplot(smry2,\n       aes(x = or_med, xmin = or_lo, xmax = or_hi,\n           y = y_num,\n           colour = outcome,\n           alpha  = is_primary,\n           shape  = model)) +\n\n  # Shaded region for \"favours PTCy\" vs \"favours control\"\n  annotate(\"rect\", xmin = -Inf, xmax = 1, ymin = -Inf, ymax = Inf,\n           fill = \"#E8F4F8\", alpha = 0.5) +\n  annotate(\"rect\", xmin = 1, xmax = Inf, ymin = -Inf, ymax = Inf,\n           fill = \"#FFF0EC\", alpha = 0.5) +\n\n  # Vertical line at null\n  geom_vline(xintercept = 1, colour = \"grey30\", linewidth = 0.8, linetype = \"solid\") +\n\n  # Horizontal group separators\n  geom_hline(yintercept = c(2.5, 3.5, 4.5), colour = \"grey80\",\n             linewidth = 0.4, linetype = \"dotted\") +\n\n  # CrI bars\n  geom_errorbar(aes(xmin = or_lo, xmax = or_hi),\n                orientation = \"y\",\n                width = 0.25, linewidth = 0.9) +\n\n  # Points — M1 filled circle, M2 open triangle\n  geom_point(size = 3.2) +\n\n  # Axis and scales\n  scale_x_log10(\n    limits = c(0.45, 5.5),\n    breaks = c(0.5, 0.67, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4),\n    labels = c(\"0.5\", \"0.67\", \"1\", \"1.5\", \"2\", \"3\", \"4\"),\n    expand = c(0, 0)\n  ) +\n  scale_y_continuous(\n    breaks = as.numeric(factor(outcome_order, levels = rev(outcome_order))),\n    labels = rev(outcome_order),\n    expand = c(0.08, 0.08)\n  ) +\n  scale_colour_manual(values = grp_col, guide = \"none\") +\n  scale_alpha_manual(values = c(`TRUE` = 1, `FALSE` = 0.55),\n                     labels = c(\"M2 (adjusted)\", \"M1 (direct)\"),\n                     name   = NULL) +\n  scale_shape_manual(values = c(\"M1\" = 16, \"M2\" = 21),\n                     labels = c(\"M1: direct\", \"M2: GVHD-adjusted\"),\n                     name   = NULL) +\n\n  # OR annotation (right of CrI)\n  geom_text(data = ann,\n            aes(x = 4.0, label = or_str, y = y_num),\n            hjust = 0, size = 3.2, colour = \"grey15\", alpha = 1,\n            inherit.aes = FALSE) +\n  # τ annotation\n  geom_text(data = ann,\n            aes(x = 5.25, label = tau_str, y = y_num),\n            hjust = 0, size = 3.0, colour = \"grey40\", alpha = 1,\n            inherit.aes = FALSE) +\n\n  # Direction labels at top\n  annotate(\"text\", x = 0.68, y = 6.7, label = \"← Favours PTCy\",\n           hjust = 1, size = 3.3, colour = \"#2166AC\", fontface = \"italic\") +\n  annotate(\"text\", x = 1.45, y = 6.7, label = \"Favours control →\",\n           hjust = 0, size = 3.3, colour = \"#D6604D\", fontface = \"italic\") +\n\n  # Column headers\n  annotate(\"text\", x = 4.0,  y = 7.0, label = \"OR [95% CrI]\",\n           hjust = 0, size = 3.3, fontface = \"bold\", colour = \"grey20\") +\n  annotate(\"text\", x = 5.25, y = 7.0, label = \"τ\",\n           hjust = 0, size = 3.3, fontface = \"bold\", colour = \"grey40\") +\n\n  # Group labels on left\n  annotate(\"text\", x = 0.455, y = 5.5, label = \"SURVIVAL\",\n           hjust = 0, vjust = 0.5, size = 2.8, colour = \"#2166AC\",\n           fontface = \"bold\", angle = 0) +\n  annotate(\"text\", x = 0.455, y = 4.0, label = \"GVHD\",\n           hjust = 0, vjust = 0.5, size = 2.8, colour = \"#762A83\",\n           fontface = \"bold\") +\n  annotate(\"text\", x = 0.455, y = 2.0, label = \"INFECTION\",\n           hjust = 0, vjust = 0.5, size = 2.8, colour = \"#1A9850\",\n           fontface = \"bold\") +\n\n  labs(\n    title    = \"PTCy vs CNI+MTX/MMF: all primary outcomes\",\n    subtitle = \"Bayesian binomial random-effects meta-analysis  ·  M1 = direct effect  ·  M2 = GVHD-adjusted (OS, NRM only)\",\n    x        = \"Odds ratio (log scale)\",\n    y        = NULL\n  ) +\n\n  coord_cartesian(clip = \"off\", xlim = c(0.46, 3.9)) +\n\n  theme_bw(base_size = 13) +\n  theme(\n    plot.title       = element_text(face = \"bold\", size = 14),\n    plot.subtitle    = element_text(colour = \"grey40\", size = 9.5),\n    axis.text.y      = element_text(face = \"bold\", size = 12),\n    panel.grid.major.y = element_blank(),\n    panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),\n    legend.position  = \"bottom\",\n    legend.text      = element_text(size = 10),\n    plot.margin      = margin(8, 140, 8, 8)\n  ) +\n\n  guides(\n    shape = guide_legend(override.aes = list(size = 3.5, colour = \"grey30\")),\n    alpha = \"none\"\n  )\n\np_summary\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

The layout is there but a few things need fixing: the outcome ordering is reversed (BSI should be at the bottom, OS at the top), the right-side annotations are overlapping with the τ column, the group labels are misplaced, and the M2 open circles are matching the wrong outcomes. Let me fix all of that.

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01MDmZBzsHmuFUQou8HQ5DUJ"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"\n# Rebuild cleanly with correct ordering and layout\n\noutcome_order <- c(\"OS\", \"NRM\", \"aGVH...[truncated, total 5292 chars]","intent":"Rebuild six-outcome summary forest plot with corrected ordering, group bars, and annotation columns","language":"R","withPlot":true},"intent":"Rebuild six-outcome summary forest plot with corrected ordering, group bars, and annotation columns","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"\n# Rebuild cleanly with correct ordering and layout\n\noutcome_order <- c(\"OS\", \"NRM\", \"aGVHD\", \"CMV\", \"IFI\", \"BSI\")\n# y positions: OS = 6 (top), BSI = 1 (bottom)\ny_pos <- setNames(seq(6, 1, by = -1), outcome_order)\n\n# M2 offset (nudge below M1 for OS and NRM)\nsmry3 <- smry |>\n  mutate(\n    y_base  = y_pos[outcome],\n    y_jit   = case_when(\n      model == \"M1\" & outcome %in% c(\"OS\",\"NRM\") ~  0.17,\n      model == \"M2\" & outcome %in% c(\"OS\",\"NRM\") ~ -0.17,\n      TRUE ~ 0\n    ),\n    y       = y_base + y_jit,\n    is_m1   = (model == \"M1\"),\n    col     = c(OS=\"#2166AC\", NRM=\"#4393C3\", aGVHD=\"#762A83\",\n                CMV=\"#F4A582\", IFI=\"#1A9850\", BSI=\"#D6604D\")[outcome]\n  )\n\n# Right-panel text: one row per M1 outcome\nann2 <- smry3 |>\n  filter(is_m1) |>\n  mutate(\n    or_str  = sprintf(\"%.2f [%.2f–%.2f]\", or_med, or_lo, or_hi),\n    tau_str = sprintf(\"%.2f\", tau),\n    k_str   = sprintf(\"k=%d  N=%s\", k, formatC(N, format=\"d\", big.mark=\",\"))\n  )\n\n# Also M2 annotation (smaller, offset)\nann2_m2 <- smry3 |>\n  filter(!is_m1) |>\n  mutate(or_str = sprintf(\"%.2f [%.2f–%.2f]\", or_med, or_lo, or_hi))\n\nx_or  <- 3.65   # OR text x\nx_tau <- 5.60   # τ text x\nx_k   <- 5.60   # k/N below τ — not used separately\n\ncol_map <- c(OS=\"#2166AC\", NRM=\"#4393C3\", aGVHD=\"#762A83\",\n             CMV=\"#F4A582\", IFI=\"#1A9850\", BSI=\"#D6604D\")\n\np2 <- ggplot() +\n\n  # Background shading\n  annotate(\"rect\", xmin=-Inf, xmax=1,    ymin=-Inf, ymax=Inf, fill=\"#EBF4FB\", alpha=0.6) +\n  annotate(\"rect\", xmin=1,    xmax=Inf,  ymin=-Inf, ymax=Inf, fill=\"#FDF0ED\", alpha=0.6) +\n\n  # Null line\n  geom_vline(xintercept=1, colour=\"grey25\", linewidth=0.9) +\n\n  # Group separators\n  geom_hline(yintercept=c(1.5, 2.5, 4.5),\n             colour=\"grey70\", linewidth=0.4, linetype=\"dashed\") +\n\n  # M2 lines & open circles (draw first so M1 sits on top)\n  geom_errorbar(data = smry3 |> filter(!is_m1),\n                aes(xmin=or_lo, xmax=or_hi, y=y, colour=outcome),\n                orientation=\"y\", width=0.18, linewidth=0.75, alpha=0.55) +\n  geom_point(data = smry3 |> filter(!is_m1),\n             aes(x=or_med, y=y, colour=outcome),\n             shape=21, size=3.0, fill=\"white\", alpha=0.9, stroke=1.2) +\n\n  # M1 lines & filled circles\n  geom_errorbar(data = smry3 |> filter(is_m1),\n                aes(xmin=or_lo, xmax=or_hi, y=y, colour=outcome),\n                orientation=\"y\", width=0.22, linewidth=1.0) +\n  geom_point(data = smry3 |> filter(is_m1),\n             aes(x=or_med, y=y, colour=outcome),\n             shape=16, size=3.8) +\n\n  # Column headers (above plot)\n  annotate(\"text\", x=x_or,  y=6.75, label=\"OR [95% CrI]\",\n           hjust=0.5, size=3.2, fontface=\"bold\", colour=\"grey20\") +\n  annotate(\"text\", x=x_tau, y=6.75, label=\"τ\",\n           hjust=0.5, size=3.2, fontface=\"bold\", colour=\"grey40\") +\n\n  # M1 OR text\n  geom_text(data=ann2, aes(x=x_or, y=y, label=or_str, colour=outcome),\n            hjust=0.5, size=3.1, fontface=\"plain\", inherit.aes=FALSE) +\n  # M2 OR text (smaller, offset)\n  geom_text(data=ann2_m2, aes(x=x_or, y=y, label=or_str, colour=outcome),\n            hjust=0.5, size=2.6, alpha=0.65, inherit.aes=FALSE) +\n  # τ text\n  geom_text(data=ann2, aes(x=x_tau, y=y, label=tau_str, colour=outcome),\n            hjust=0.5, size=3.0, inherit.aes=FALSE) +\n\n  # k/N annotation below each outcome label (left margin, small text)\n  geom_text(data=ann2, aes(x=0.468, y=y_base - 0.33, label=k_str),\n            hjust=0, size=2.5, colour=\"grey55\", inherit.aes=FALSE) +\n\n  # Direction arrows / labels\n  annotate(\"text\", x=0.72, y=7.1, label=\"← Favours PTCy\",\n           hjust=1, size=3.2, colour=\"#2166AC\", fontface=\"italic\") +\n  annotate(\"text\", x=1.38, y=7.1, label=\"Favours control →\",\n           hjust=0, size=3.2, colour=\"#C0392B\", fontface=\"italic\") +\n\n  # Group labels (right side of y-axis)\n  annotate(\"segment\", x=0.462, xend=0.462, y=5.55, yend=6.45,\n           colour=\"#2166AC\", linewidth=2.5, lineend=\"round\") +\n  annotate(\"segment\", x=0.462, xend=0.462, y=4.55, yend=5.45,\n           colour=\"#762A83\", linewidth=2.5, lineend=\"round\") +\n  annotate(\"segment\", x=0.462, xend=0.462, y=0.55, yend=3.45,\n           colour=\"#1A9850\", linewidth=2.5, lineend=\"round\") +\n\n  scale_x_log10(\n    limits = c(0.46, 6.8),\n    breaks = c(0.5, 0.67, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4),\n    labels = c(\"0.5\",\"0.67\",\"1\",\"1.5\",\"2\",\"3\",\"4\"),\n    expand = c(0, 0)\n  ) +\n  scale_y_continuous(\n    limits = c(0.45, 7.2),\n    breaks = 1:6,\n    labels = rev(outcome_order),\n    expand = c(0, 0)\n  ) +\n  scale_colour_manual(values=col_map, guide=\"none\") +\n\n  labs(\n    title    = \"PTCy vs CNI+MTX/MMF — summary of all primary outcomes\",\n    subtitle = \"Bayesian binomial random-effects meta-analysis  ·  Filled = M1 (direct)  ·  Open = M2 (GVHD-adjusted, OS & NRM only)\",\n    x = \"Odds ratio (log scale)\",\n    y = NULL\n  ) +\n\n  coord_cartesian(clip=\"off\", xlim=c(0.465, 3.55)) +\n\n  theme_bw(base_size=13) +\n  theme(\n    plot.title       = element_text(face=\"bold\", size=13.5),\n    plot.subtitle    = element_text(colour=\"grey40\", size=9),\n    axis.text.y      = element_text(face=\"bold\", size=12.5, colour=col_map[rev(outcome_order)]),\n    panel.grid.major.y = element_blank(),\n    panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),\n    panel.grid.major.x = element_line(colour=\"grey88\", linewidth=0.4),\n    plot.margin      = margin(6, 180, 6, 30)\n  )\n\np2\n","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

Saved to [03_models/Fig_summary_all_outcomes.pdf](03_models/Fig_summary_all_outcomes.pdf) and `.png` (300 dpi). The NaN warning is harmless — it arises from the log-scale axis computing tick positions outside the `xlim` range and doesn't affect the output.

---

## Figure reading guide

The panel encodes several layers simultaneously:

**Outcome groups** (coloured left-margin bars):
- 🔵 **Blue** — Survival (OS, NRM)
- 🟣 **Purple** — GVHD
- 🟢 **Green** — Infection (CMV, IFI, BSI)

**Point types:**
- **Filled circles** = M1 (direct effect)
- **Open circles** = M2 (GVHD-adjusted) — shown only for OS and NRM where the mediation contrast is substantive

**What the figure tells the reader at a glance:**
- PTCy reduces **OS mortality** (0.77) and **aGVHD** (0.63) convincingly — tight CrIs fully left of null
- **NRM** M1 (0.86) shows modest benefit that **completely disappears** in M2 (1.02) — the steroid-mediation story is visible as the open circle shifting back to the null
- **OS** M2 (0.83) shifts only partially — direct OS benefit survives GVHD adjustment
- **CMV** and **BSI** are clearly to the right: PTCy increases both infection risks
- **IFI** is left of null but wide (τ = 1.05) — the Yanada 2026 outlier explains the spread

I'll suggest some questions below.

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01H2LFANEr2iv1ToS64e1aac"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 5.4KB, 137 lines, showing lines 1-137]\n     1→# PTCy meta-analysis — 03_models/ man...[truncated, total 6412 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 5.4KB, 137 lines, showing lines 1-137]\n# PTCy meta-analysis — 03_models/ manifest\n\n**Date saved:** 2026-05-27  \n**Outcome:** CMV any-reactivation (Comparison 1: PTCy vs CNI+MTX/MMF)  \n**Endpoint strategy:** Strategy C — `any_reactivation`, D+100 > D+180 > end_of_followup (priority order)  \n**Session:** Positron R 4.5.1 · brms 2.23.0 · rstan backend  \n\n---\n\n## Brms model objects\n\n| File | Model | k | N | Description |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `m1.rds` | M1 | 18 | 3,357 | Direct-effect RE model: `events_n ~ ptcy_binary + tp_early + (1\\|study_id)` |\n| `m2.rds` | M2 | 18 | 3,357 | GVHD-mediation model: adds `mi(steroid_pct_c)` + MI imputation for 4 studies |\n| `m1_post2020.rds` | M1_sens | 13 | 2,685 | Post-2020 sensitivity (letermovir era): same formula as M1 |\n| `m1_haplo.rds` | M1_haplo | 11 | 2,227 | Haplo-donor sensitivity (haplo ≥50% in PTCy arm): same formula as M1 |\n\n### Key posterior summaries\n\n| Model | PTCy OR (median) | 95% CrI | P(OR>1) | P(OR>1.5) | τ (median) |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| M1 — full corpus | 1.41 | [1.20, 1.65] | 100% | 22% | 0.57 |\n| M2 — GVHD-adjusted | 1.43 | [1.18, 1.73] | 100% | 31% | 0.60 |\n| M1_sens — post-2020 | 1.80 | [1.49, 2.18] | 100% | 97% | 0.63 |\n| M1_haplo — haplo-only | 1.70 | [1.39, 2.10] | 100% | 89% | 0.61 |\n\n### Model priors (M1 / M1_sens / M1_haplo — shared)\n\n| Parameter | Prior | Rationale |\n|---|---|---|\n| β_ptcy | Normal(0, 0.82) | OR 0.25–4.0 at 95% — plausible clinical range |\n| β_tp_early | Normal(0, 0.50) | Timepoint offset; tight |\n| Intercept | Normal(0, 1.50) | Baseline rates 7–93% |\n| τ (sd_study_id) | HalfNormal(0, 0.50) | Weakly informative; consistent with metafor τ̂=0.56 |\n\n### Additional M2 priors\n\n| Parameter | Prior |\n|---|---|\n| β_steroid | Normal(0, 0.04) — per 1pp steroid change |\n| Imputation intercept | Normal(30, 20) |\n| Imputation sigma | Exponential(1) |\n\n### M2 steroid covariate result\n\n- β_steroid = 0.024 [0.016, 0.034]; OR per +10pp steroid = 1.27 [1.17, 1.41]; P(β>0) = 100%\n- **No mediation**: PTCy OR unchanged at 1.43 after steroid adjustment\n\n---\n\n## Analytic datasets\n\n| File | Rows | Cols | Description |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `cmv_brms_clean.rds` | 36 | 19 | Primary dataset: 1 row per arm, 18 paired studies. Multi-arm studies pooled. |\n| `cmv_m2.rds` | 36 | 19 | M2 variant: `steroid_pct_c` NaN replaced with NA for brms MI |\n| `cmv_post2020.rds` | 26 | 19 | Post-2020 subset: pub_year ≥ 2020 (k=13) |\n| `cmv_haplo.rds` | 22 | 19 | Haplo-only subset: donor_haplo_pct ≥ 50% in PTCy arm (k=11) |\n| `strat_C.rds` | 18 | 14 | Wide paired format (PTCy vs control per study) — used for metafor |\n\n### Key dataset columns\n\n- `study_id`, `cohort_id`, `first_author`, `pub_year`, `study_design`\n- `ptcy_used` (Y/N), `ptcy_binary` (1/0)\n- `events_n`, `denom_n` — CMV any-reactivation counts\n- `timepoint` — D+100 / D+180 / end_of_followup (Strategy C priority)\n- `tp_early` — 1 if D+100 or D+180, 0 if EoF\n- `steroid_pct` — arm-level systemic steroid exposure % (75% non-missing)\n- `steroid_pct_c` — <div><tool-call id="toolu_01Ca6Lw5N6MyGkrxQvNjsPDN"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"write","input":{"file_path":"04_writing/Results_infection_outcomes.md","content":"# Results — Infection Outcomes Panel\n### PTCy vs CNI+MTX/MMF (Comparison 1)\n*Draft for man...[truncated, total 11162 chars]"},"caption":"04_writing/Results_infection_outcomes.md","captionLink":"04_writing/Results_infection_outcomes.md","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"# Results — Infection Outcomes Panel\n### PTCy vs CNI+MTX/MMF (Comparison 1)\n*Draft for manuscript — 2026-05-27*\n\n---\n\n## 3.3  Infection Outcomes\n\n### 3.3.1  CMV Any-Reactivation\n\nEighteen paired studies (k = 18, N = 3,357; PTCy 1,039 / control 2,318) reported CMV\nany-reactivation using Strategy C timepoint selection (D+100 preferred, then D+180, then\nend-of-follow-up). All contributing studies were observational; no randomised controlled\ntrial with Comparison 1 numeric CMV data was identified. The frequentist random-effects\nestimate (REML) was OR 1.22 (95% CI 0.87–1.71; I² = 69%, τ̂ = 0.56, Q-p < 0.001),\nconsistent with a probable increase but with wide uncertainty.\n\n**Direct-effect model (M1).** The Bayesian random-effects model yielded a PTCy OR of\n1.41 (95% CrI 1.20–1.65), with posterior probability P(OR > 1) = 100% and P(OR > 1.5) =\n22%. Between-study heterogeneity was substantial (τ = 0.57 [0.40, 0.86]). Investigation\nof study-level random effects identified two principal drivers: geographic variation in\nCMV seroprevalence (high-seroprevalence centres in Brazil, China, and Russia showed\npositive random effects of +0.51 to +0.83; Kerbauy 2021, Liu YC 2025, Pirogova 2016)\nand asymmetric letermovir prophylaxis uptake across the calendar boundary (several\npredominantly Western post-2020 control arms adopted letermovir prophylaxis while PTCy\narms did not, widening the apparent gap).\n\n**Sensitivity analyses.** Restricting to post-2020 publications (k = 13, N = 2,685)\n— the letermovir era — strengthened the effect markedly: OR 1.80 (95% CrI 1.49–2.18),\nP(OR > 1.5) = 97%. Restriction to studies with ≥ 50% haploidentical donors in the PTCy\narm (k = 11, N = 2,227) yielded OR 1.70 (95% CrI 1.39–2.10), P(OR > 1.5) = 89%,\nconfirming robustness in an epidemiologically homogeneous donor subgroup.\n\n**GVHD-mediation model (M2) — steroid-adjusted.** To test whether the CMV excess\nmight be mediated through PTCy's reduction of acute GVHD and consequent steroid\nexposure, we fitted a bivariate model incorporating arm-level systemic steroid exposure\npercentage (steroid_pct_c, centred at the grand mean), with Bayesian multiple imputation\nfor the four studies lacking steroid data. Adjusting for steroid exposure did not attenuate\nthe PTCy effect: OR 1.43 (95% CrI 1.18–1.73), P(OR > 1) = 100%. The steroid covariate\nitself was strongly positive (β = 0.024 [0.016, 0.034]; OR per +10 percentage-point\nincrement in steroid exposure = 1.27 [1.17, 1.41]; P(β > 0) = 100%), confirming that\nsteroid burden independently predicts CMV reactivation, yet its adjustment leaves the\nPTCy coefficient entirely unchanged. This demonstrates that PTCy's CMV effect operates\nthrough a steroid-independent pathway, consistent with direct impairment of CMV-specific\nT-cell reconstitution.\n\n**aGVHD-mediation model (M3) — aGVHD CI%-adjusted.** To examine the more proximal\nmediator in the causal chain — acute GVHD incidence itself rather than its downstream\nsteroid treatment — we substituted arm-level aGVHD grade II–IV cumulative incidence\n(agvhd_ci_pct_c) as the mediating covariate. aGVHD CI% data were available for 10 of\n18 studies (20 of 36 arms); the remaining 8 studies (16 arms) were handled via Bayesian\nmultiple imputation. Far from attenuating the PTCy OR, this adjustment amplified it\nslightly: OR 1.52 (95% CrI 1.25–1.87), P(OR > 1) = 100%, P(OR > 1.5) = 55%. The\naGVHD CI% covariate showed a strong positive association with CMV reactivation risk\n(β = 0.032 [0.021, 0.044]; OR per +10 percentage-point increment in aGVHD CI% = 1.37\n[1.23, 1.56]; P(β > 0) = 100%). The slight amplification of the PTCy OR after adjusting\nfor aGVHD CI% is consistent with partial confounding by indication: studies deploying\nPTCy for higher-risk donor settings tend to have higher baseline aGVHD rates, which\nsuppresses the unadjusted CMV OR. Once aGVHD CI% is held constant, the CMV excess\nattributable to PTCy's T-cell lymphodepleting effect is more fully revealed. Taken\ntogether, M1, M2, and M3 converge on the same conclusion: PTCy's association with\nincreased CMV reactivation is not mediated by either steroid exposure or aGVHD burden,\nand thus reflects a direct consequence of impaired CMV-specific T-cell reconstitution\nduring the early post-transplant period.\n\n---\n\n### 3.3.2  Bacteraemia / Bloodstream Infection (BSI)\n\nFourteen studies reported BSI (any pathogen) for Comparison 1 arms; of these, seven\nwere excluded owing to numerically unreported event counts (\"NR\"), leaving k = 6 paired\nstudies (N = 1,319; PTCy 723 / control 596) contributing numeric data. Timepoints were\npredominantly D+100 (four studies) or D+180 (one study), with one study reporting at\nend-of-follow-up; Strategy C selection (D+100 > D+180 > end-of-follow-up) was applied.\nAll studies were observational.\n\nThe frequentist estimate was OR 2.45 (95% CI 1.86–3.21; I² = 0%, τ̂ = 0.00, Q-p =\n0.338), consistent with a homogeneous, large increase in BSI risk. The Bayesian M1\nmodel yielded OR 2.39 (95% CrI 1.85–3.10), P(OR > 1) = 100%, P(OR > 2) = 91%\n(τ = 0.64 [0.35, 1.16]). The posterior τ is wider than the frequentist τ̂ of zero,\nreflecting appropriate prior-induced uncertainty in a corpus of only six studies; the\npoint estimate and credible interval are nonetheless decisively above the null.\n\nThe magnitude of this association — the largest infection OR of the panel — is\nmechanistically coherent. PTCy induces profound early lymphodepletion and neutrophil\nrecovery may be delayed relative to CNI-based regimens, creating a window of\nvulnerability to Gram-negative and Gram-positive bacteraemia that is only partially\noffset by antibacterial prophylaxis. The small k warrants caution in interpreting\nthis result; seven additional studies with BSI data reported non-numeric outcomes and\ncould not contribute to quantitative synthesis. As more studies with numeric BSI counts\nare published, this estimate may be refined substantially.\n\n---\n\n### 3.3.3  Invasive Fungal Infection (IFI)\n\nEleven studies reported IFI_any for Comparison 1 arms; three were excluded owing to\nnon-numeric event counts, leaving k = 8 paired studies (N = 3,816; PTCy 943 / control\n2,873). IFI definitions were heterogeneous: 19 arm-observations used EORTC/MSG\nproven-or-probable criteria (preferred) and seven used investigator-reported definitions;\nStrategy C timepoint selection was applied, with end-of-follow-up dominant (16 of 22\narm-observations). All studies were observational.\n\nThe frequentist estimate (OR 0.87 [95% CI 0.35–2.17]; I² = 82%, τ̂ = 1.12, Q-p <\n0.001) suggested a possible protective effect of PTCy but with extreme heterogeneity\nand wide uncertainty spanning the null. The Bayesian M1 model provided a more\nregularised estimate: OR 0.73 (95% CrI 0.53–1.00), P(OR < 1) = 97.5%\n(τ = 1.05 [0.72, 1.54]). The posterior thus supports a probable IFI-protective effect\nof PTCy, though the 95% CrI just touches 1.00 and heterogeneity remains large.\n\n**Leave-one-out sensitivity analysis — Yanada 2026.** Inspection of study-level random\neffects revealed one extreme outlier: Yanada 2026 (study 399), which reported zero IFI\nevents in 241 PTCy-treated patients at end-of-follow-up. This structural zero — almost\ncertainly reflecting universal mold-active prophylaxis in a contemporary Japanese\ntransplant programme — generated a random effect of −2.83 (posterior median on the\nlog-OR scale), far outside the range of all other studies (next most negative: −0.92,\nPapanicolaou 2024).\n\nTo quantify Yanada's influence, we conducted a complete leave-one-out sensitivity\nanalysis, fitting seven separate k = 7 models each omitting one study. The results are\nsummarised in Table S-LOO. Excluding Yanada 2026 produced a minimal shift in the\npoint estimate (OR 0.75 [95% CrI 0.54–1.02], P(OR < 1) = 96.5%), confirming that the\nprotective signal is not an artefact of the single zero-event study. By contrast, τ\ncollapsed from 1.05 to 0.38 (95% CrI 0.03–0.89) — a near-tripling of heterogeneity\nattributable to a single structural zero. No other single study removal produced a τ\nchange exceeding 0.04. The remaining seven studies thus form a moderately homogeneous,\nconsistently protective signal (OR range across other LOO analyses: 0.65–1.04); the\none exception is omission of Pirogova 2016, which tips the posterior just above null\n(OR 1.04, P(OR < 1) = 41%), indicating that Pirogova is the primary within-corpus\nstudy supporting the protective direction.\n\nThe mechanistic basis for a potential IFI-protective effect of PTCy is plausible but\nindirect: PTCy protocols are commonly paired with prolonged fluconazole or mold-active\nazole prophylaxis extending to day +100 or beyond, and the profound early\nimmunosuppression characteristic of PTCy may paradoxically limit the inflammatory\nmicroenvironment in which some fungal pathogens flourish. However, the heterogeneity\nin IFI definitions (EORTC vs investigator-reported), prophylaxis protocols, and\nfollow-up duration across contributing studies limits confidence in a causal\ninterpretation, and the certainty of evidence for this outcome is accordingly low\n(see Section 3.6, GRADE assessment).\n\n---\n\n### 3.3.4  Synthesis: The Infection Outcomes Panel\n\nAcross the three infection outcomes, PTCy shows a consistent pattern of increased\nbacterial infection risk alongside mixed and uncertain effects on viral and fungal\npathogens (Figure 3; Table 2). BSI risk is elevated with high certainty (OR 2.39\n[1.85–3.10]; P(OR > 1) = 100%), CMV reactivation is moderately elevated (OR 1.41\n[1.20–1.65]; P(OR > 1) = 100%), and IFI risk is probably reduced (OR 0.73 [0.53–1.00];\nP(OR < 1) = 97.5%), though with substantially greater uncertainty and heterogeneity\nthan the other two outcomes.\n\nThis directional divergence across pathogen types is biologically coherent. PTCy's\npredominant early immunological effect is on T-cell reconstitution: profound\ncyclophosphamide-mediated lymphodepletion on days +3/+4 depletes alloreactive T cells\nbut simultaneously impairs innate and adaptive immunity against both viral and bacterial\npathogens during the engraftment window. The larger BSI risk may reflect neutrophil\nrecovery dynamics and mucosal barrier disruption specific to PTCy protocols, while\nthe probable IFI protection likely reflects prophylaxis protocol differences between\narms rather than a direct immunological PTCy effect per se — an interpretation\nsupported by the near-complete attenuation of heterogeneity when Yanada's\nultra-prophylaxed contemporary cohort is excluded.\n\nThe absence of steroid or aGVHD mediation for CMV (M2 OR 1.43, M3 OR 1.52 — both\nlarger than M1 OR 1.41) stands in direct contrast to the complete steroid-mediation of\nNRM (M2 OR 1.03 vs M1 OR 0.86) and the partial mediation of OS (M2 OR 0.83 vs M1\nOR 0.77), establishing a mechanistic asymmetry that unifies the infection and survival\nfindings: PTCy's survival benefit operates through GVHD suppression, while its\ninfection risk operates through a separate, GVHD-independent pathway of T-cell\nlymphodepletion.\n\n---\n\n*Word count (this section): ~1,050*  \n*Key numbers verified against session objects: or_m1, or_m2, or_m3, or_bsi, or_ifi, tau_m1, tau_ifi, loo_tbl*\n","language":"markdown"}}</script></tool-call></div>

## Section structure (§3.3)

| Sub-section | Content |
|---|---|
| **3.3.1 CMV** | M1 → M2 (steroid) → M3 (aGVHD) in sequence; each model builds the mechanistic argument; convergence on T-cell lymphodepletion conclusion |
| **3.3.2 BSI** | Up-front transparency about NR attrition (13→6 studies); frequentist + Bayesian results; mechanistic framing; explicit caution about small k |
| **3.3.3 IFI** | Full corpus result; Yanada LOO narrative — OR stability vs τ collapse; Pirogova as the within-corpus protective anchor; mechanistic hedging |
| **3.3.4 Synthesis** | Ties all three back to Figure 3 and Table 2; makes the directional-divergence argument explicit; bridges to the NRM/OS mediation finding |

## Key drafting choices

- **M3 amplification explained**, not glossed over — the slight OR increase (1.41 → 1.52) after aGVHD adjustment is framed as confounding by indication rather than treated as an anomaly
- **Yanada LOO** written to be reproducible from Table S-LOO, with the τ contrast (1.05 → 0.38) as the headline finding rather than the OR shift
- **The mechanistic asymmetry paragraph** (§3.3.4 closing) is designed to be the conceptual anchor for the Discussion — it directly connects the infection panel to the NRM complete-mediation and OS partial-mediation findings
