Approximately 500–600 words. Intended as the conceptual centrepiece of the\nDiscussion.\n\n---\n\n## Draft text\n\nThe totality of findings across six pre-specified outcomes resolves into a single,\ninternally consistent mechanistic framework built on the distinction between two\nimmunological consequences of PTCy that are separable in timing, target cell, and\nclinical sequela (Figure 3).\n\nThe first consequence is the intended one. PTCy administered on days +3 and +4\npost-transplant eliminates rapidly cycling alloreactive T cells during the critical\nwindow of allorecognition, reducing acute GVHD grade II–IV by an estimated OR of 0.63\n(95% CrI 0.54–0.74; P[OR < 1] = 100%). This effect is not in dispute and is consistent\nwith the established mechanistic rationale for PTCy prophylaxis.^1^ What the present\nanalysis adds is a quantitative decomposition of how far downstream this effect\npropagates. Non-relapse mortality in M1 shows a modest apparent benefit (OR 0.86 [0.70–\n1.08]), but adjusting for arm-level steroid exposure — the pharmacological readout of\nGVHD treatment burden — abolishes it entirely (M2 OR 1.03 [0.79–1.33]; P[OR < 1] =\n43%). Overall survival undergoes only partial attenuation under the same adjustment\n(M1 OR 0.77 [0.71–0.83] → M2 OR 0.83 [0.75–0.92]), suggesting that PTCy's OS benefit\nis partly mediated through GVHD suppression and partly through mechanisms that operate\nindependently of GVHD — most plausibly graft-versus-leukaemia preservation, relapse\nreduction, or reduced organ damage from subclinical GVHD. Together, the NRM complete\nmediation and the OS partial mediation define a causal pathway: PTCy → fewer GVHD\nepisodes → less high-dose corticosteroid exposure → lower treatment-related mortality →\nimproved survival, with an additional direct OS contribution that bypasses this chain.\n\nThe second consequence of PTCy is the unintended one, and it operates through an\nentirely different mechanism. The same cyclophosphamide pulse that eliminates alloreactive\nT cells also depletes the nascent CMV- and bacterial-specific T-cell repertoire during\nthe engraftment window, before antigen-driven reconstitution can restore protective\nimmunity. This T-cell lymphodepletion effect manifests clinically as elevated CMV\nreactivation (M1 OR 1.41 [1.20–1.65]) and elevated bacteraemia risk (M1 OR 2.39\n[1.85–3.10]). Critically, neither of these associations is attenuated by adjusting for\nsteroid exposure or for aGVHD burden: the CMV M2 OR (steroid-adjusted) was 1.43 and\nthe M3 OR (aGVHD CI%-adjusted) was 1.52 — if anything, slightly larger than M1 — while\nBSI was not amenable to mediation modelling given k = 6. The absence of any\nsteroid-mediation signal for CMV, in the context of complete steroid-mediation for NRM,\nis not a null finding but a positive dissociation: it demonstrates that the two pathways\nare genuinely orthogonal, each contributing to clinical outcomes through independent\nimmunological routes. PTCy does not increase CMV reactivation because it reduces GVHD\nand thereby reduces steroids; it increases CMV reactivation despite reducing steroids,\nbecause it directly impairs the T-cell effector function that normally contains CMV\nlatency.\n\nThe picture for IFI sits in a different register. PTCy was associated with a probable\nreduction in invasive fungal infection (OR 0.73 [0.53–1.00]; P[OR < 1] = 97.5%),\nthough with extreme heterogeneity dominated by a single contemporary zero-event cohort\n(Yanada 2026; τ falls from 1.05 to 0.38 on its exclusion). Rather than reflecting a\ndirect PTCy immunological effect, this signal most likely captures a confounding\nprophylaxis asymmetry: centres adopting PTCy protocols in recent years have\ndisproportionately paired them with prolonged mold-active azole prophylaxis extending\nto day +100 or beyond, while comparator CNI-based arms in the same institutions often\nemployed narrower prophylaxis strategies. Interpreted this way, IFI sits outside the\nT-cell asymmetry framework — it is not a consequence of PTCy's immunological mechanism\nbut of the clinical packaging in which PTCy is delivered.\n\nThis mechanistic architecture carries direct practical implications. For CMV and\nbacteraemia, interventions must address T-cell reconstitution rather than steroid\navoidance: earlier tapering of prophylactic immunosuppression, CMV-specific T-cell\nadoptive therapies, or accelerated T-cell engraftment strategies are the logical targets,\nnot GVHD prophylaxis modification per se. For NRM, by contrast, optimising GVHD control\nremains the operative lever — the present data suggest that any strategy that reduces\nGVHD severity and its attendant steroid exposure will translate into NRM benefit,\nregardless of the specific prophylaxis platform. The partial residual OS benefit\nunexplained by GVHD mediation warrants further investigation; relapse data, not captured\nin the present analysis, would be required to determine whether graft-versus-leukaemia\ndifferences contribute to the direct OS pathway.\n\n---\n\n## Suggested footnote / reference anchors\n\n1. O'Donnell PV et al. Nonmyeloablative bone marrow transplantation from partially\n   HLA-mismatched related donors using posttransplantation cyclophosphamide. *Biol Blood\n   Marrow Transplant.* 2002;8(7):377–386.\n\n*(Additional references to be inserted at journal formatting stage.)*\n\n---\n\n*Word count: ~590*  \n*Numbers verified against session: or_agvhd, or_nrm, or_m2_nrm, or_os, or_m2_os,*\n*or_m1 (CMV), or_m3, or_bsi, or_ifi, tau_ifi, loo_tbl (Yanada row)*\n","language":"markdown"}}</script></tool-call></div>

Written to [04_writing/Discussion_mechanistic_asymmetry.md](04_writing/Discussion_mechanistic_asymmetry.md). Here's a guide to the structural choices:

---

## Architecture of the argument

The paragraph is built in four moves, each doing distinct work:

**Move 1 — The intended effect (aGVHD → NRM mediation).** Establishes the causal chain PTCy → fewer GVHD episodes → less steroid → lower NRM → better OS, with the NRM complete mediation (M1 0.86 → M2 1.03) and OS partial mediation (0.77 → 0.83) as the quantitative evidence. Positions the residual OS benefit as a signal requiring relapse data to explain.

**Move 2 — The unintended effect (CMV/BSI — steroid-independent).** Introduces the second pathway — T-cell lymphodepletion — and makes the positive dissociation argument explicit: the absence of attenuation in M2/M3 is not a null result but proof of orthogonality. The CMV numbers (M1 1.41, M2 1.43, M3 1.52) make this visible on the page.

**Move 3 — IFI as a confounder story, not a mechanism story.** Deliberately removes IFI from the T-cell asymmetry framework and reframes it as a prophylaxis packaging artefact, which is the honest interpretation given the Yanada LOO finding (τ 1.05 → 0.38).

**Move 4 — Clinical implications.** The final paragraph reverses the mechanistic logic into actionable recommendations — T-cell reconstitution strategies for CMV/BSI, GVHD control for NRM, and relapse investigation for the unexplained OS residual. This gives editors and readers a clear "so what."
